Um... well, I always try really hard to stay on top of things, because my job and my clients are super important to me. But... I guess, yeah, sometimes it can be a little tricky?

Um... a typical workday. Well, I always want to make sure I'm doing a really good job for my clients, so I try my best to keep everything on track. I mean, advertising sales is pretty fast-paced, so there's always a lot going on. 

I guess... well, sometimes getting everything organized can be a little... challenging? But I'm sure everybody feels that way sometimes, right? It's just that... well, when I'm working on a campaign, I'll take notes in an email, and then maybe I'll jot something down on a sticky note while I'm on a call, and then I'll put something else in my actual planner... and I usually have a bunch of tabs open on my computer, too.

Um... well, outside of work, I think things are mostly okay. I really try to be present with my friends and family, like I said. But... um... I guess where it's really starting to become an issue is actually back at work, if I'm being completely honest.

was.\n- key: S003\n  name: Listening_Difficulty\n  description: Often does not seem to listen when spoken to directly.\n  manifestation: When my manager gives me feedback or a client is explaining what they want changed, I can be looking right at them and nodding, then seconds later I have to ask them to repeat the part I just missed.\n- key: S004\n  name: Instruction_Follow through_Difficulty\n  description: Often does not follow through on instructions and fails to finish schoolwork, chores, or duties in the workplace.\n  manifestation: I’ll get most of a proposal or CRM update done, but then I leave off the last step, like sending the final version or logging the follow-up, even though I meant to finish it.\n- key: S005\n  name: Organization_Difficulty\n  description: Often has difficulty organizing tasks and activities.\n  manifestation: My campaign notes end up split between emails, sticky notes, my planner, and random tabs, and I waste a lot of time figuring out what to do first instead of just starting.\n- key: S007\n  name: Losing_Items\n  description: Often loses things necessary for tasks or activities.\n  manifestation: I’m constantly hunting for things I need for work, like my headset, client one-pagers, keys, or the notebook I was using ten minutes earlier.\n- key: S011\n  name: Leaving_Seat\n  description: Often leaves seat in situations when remaining seated is expected.\n  manifestation: Honestly, what made me come in is that I keep getting up during long sales strategy meetings when I know I’m supposed to stay seated, even if it’s just to refill water or pace by the printer.\n- key: S015\n  name: Excessive_Talking\n  description: Often talks excessively.\n  manifestation: In team pitches I can hear myself going on too long about the campaign idea, and by the time I stop, I can tell other people were waiting for a chance to speak.\n- key: S016\n  name: Blurting_Answers\n  description: Often blurts out an answer before a question has been completed.\n  manifestation: I often jump in with an answer before a client or coworker has finished asking the question, and then I realize I answered the wrong thing or cut them off.\n- key: S017\n  name: Waiting_Turn_Difficulty\n  description: Often has difficulty waiting his or her turn.\n  manifestation: Waiting my turn on client calls or in office discussions makes me restless, so I keep checking the clock or rehearsing what I want to say until I blurt it out too early.\n- key: S018\n  name: Interrupting_Intruding\n  description: Often interrupts or intrudes on others (e.g., butts into conversations, games, or activities).\n  manifestation: If I think of an idea while someone else is working through a deck, I’ll pop over or interrupt their conversation to add my thought, even when it probably could have waited.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: Onset prior to age 12 years\n  manifestation: I remember this starting way before high school—my elementary school teachers wrote that I was bright but rushed, talked out of turn, forgot homework, and had a desk stuffed with months\n  manifestation: This has been noticeably affecting me since I changed into advertising sales strategy about two years and two months ago.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 30 / advertising_sales_agent / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S011\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Can you describe a typical workday for me? Well, I always want to make sure I'm doing a really good job for my clients, so I try my best to keep everything on track. I mean, advertising sales is pretty fast-paced, so there's always a lot going on. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes getting everything organized can be a little... challenging? But... I guess, yeah, sometimes it can be a little tricky? \n\nLike, if we're in one of those longer strategy meetings, or if I'm reading through a really big client brief... I always start out completely focused and ready to go. But then, after a few minutes, I'll kind of snap back and realize I've just been staring at the exact same slide, or the same paragraph, and I have absolutely no idea what the last point even was. \n\nI mean, everyone zones out a little bit when there's a ton of information to process, right? I really hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm slacking off or not paying attention on purpose, because I really do care about doing a good job. Is that... does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you find it hard to stay focused on things like reading, watching TV, or following conversations with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um... outside of work? No, I don't think so. I mean, I really value my relationships, so when I'm with my friends or family, I always make sure to listen to what they're saying. I'd never want them to feel like I don't care about what's going on in their lives. \n\nAnd with reading or TV... I mean, I guess if a show is just really boring, I might check my phone or something, but that's pretty normal, right? Everyone multitasks nowadays. But I'm sure everybody feels that way sometimes, right? It's just that... well, when I'm working on a campaign, I'll take notes in an email, and then maybe I'll jot something down on a sticky note while I'm on a call, and then I'll put something else in my actual planner... and I usually have a bunch of tabs open on my computer, too. \n\nSo, I guess sometimes I end up spending a bit more time than I should just trying to figure out where all my notes are and what I'm supposed to actually do first, instead of just... starting the work. I really do try to keep it all together.\n\nDoctor: Outside of the meetings at work, have you noticed that this restlessness or difficulty staying focused affects other important parts of your life, like your personal relationships or your ability to relax at home?\n\nPatient: Um... well, outside of work, I think things are mostly okay. Um... frustrated after work? Well, I mean, I think everyone has days where they feel a little discouraged if things don't go perfectly, right? But... um... I wouldn't say I get really down or anything.

Um... unusually tired? No, I really don't think so. I mean, my energy levels feel pretty normal to me. I always try to bring a lot of enthusiasm to my job, you know? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... my mind wandering? Well... I mean, I really try my hardest to stay focused, especially because my clients deserve my full attention. But... I guess, maybe sometimes? 

Like, if we're in one of those really long strategy meetings, or if I'm reading a really dense client brief... I always start out super focused, taking notes and everything! But then, after a few minutes, I'll kind of... realize I've been staring at the exact same slide or the same paragraph for a while. And I'll have absolutely no idea what the last point was. 

But I always try to catch myself and jump right back in! I don't want anyone to think I don't care about the presentation. I think it just happens because my brain is trying to process so many great ideas for the client all at once, you know? That's not too terrible, is it? I really hope it just means I'm a creative thinker. Does that sound like a normal thing to happen during long meetings?

I really do try to keep it all together.\n\nDoctor: Outside of the meetings at work, have you noticed that this restlessness or difficulty staying focused affects other important parts of your life, like your personal relationships or your ability to relax at home?\n\nPatient: Um... well, outside of work, I think things are mostly okay. I really try to be present with my friends and family, like I said. But... um... I guess where it's really starting to become an issue is actually back at work, if I'm being completely honest. \n\nI really didn't want to bring this up because I don't want it to sound like I'm bad at my job, because I really, really care about my clients. But... um... it's kind of started showing up in my performance reviews lately? Just little comments about my attention to detail. I worry so much that I might accidentally damage one of my accounts, even though I'm trying so hard to do everything right. I just want to do good work for them, you know? I hope you don't think I'm irresponsible... I really am trying my best to keep everything running smoothly. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if the difficulties you're experiencing with staying focused and feeling restless have ever caused problems in any areas of your life outside of work, such as with your personal relationships, household responsibilities, or other daily activities at home?\n\nPatient: Um... outside of work? No, I really don't think so. I mean, I try really hard to keep my personal life running smoothly. I know I mentioned that work has been a little stressful lately, but at home... um... things are pretty normal. \n\nI make sure to keep up with my chores and everything. Sometimes I might leave a basket of laundry out for a day or two, or maybe I'll start cleaning the kitchen and then get sidetracked by organizing the mail... but that's just normal, right? Everyone gets a little distracted at home when they're trying to relax after a long day. \n\nAnd with my relationships, like I said before, I really value my friends and family. I would never let my work stress or anything else get in the way of being there for them. I always try to be a good friend and a good partner. I hope that's what you were asking? I just really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days recently where you felt down, sad, or uninterested in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... down or sad? No, not really. I always try to look on the bright side of things, and I still really enjoy my hobbies when I have the time for them. I like to keep a positive attitude, especially for my clients and my friends. Does that sound okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel down, frustrated, or discouraged specifically after work, especially on days when things didn't go as planned or you felt particularly distracted?\n\nPatient: Um... frustrated after work? Um... specific tasks? Well, I mean, I always try really hard to get everything done for my clients, because they're so important to me. But... I guess maybe sometimes the... the very end of things can be a little tricky? 

Like, I'll put so much energy into getting a proposal together, or updating all the client details in the CRM system. I'll get all the hard parts done, you know? The creative stuff, the numbers... all of that is usually mostly finished. But then... um... sometimes I might just... forget to do the very last little step. 

Like, I might leave the final version sitting in my drafts instead of actually hitting send, or I'll forget to log the follow-up date in the system. Even though I fully meant to do it! It's just that my brain is already jumping ahead to the next big project or the next client I want to help. 

But it's just little things like that, and I almost always catch it eventually! I really do try my best to be thorough. I hope that doesn't make me sound careless? I just get so excited about moving forward with the work. Is it normal to sometimes overlook those tiny final details when you're really busy?

Sometimes I might leave a basket of laundry out for a day or two, or maybe I'll start cleaning the kitchen and then get sidetracked by organizing the mail... but that's just normal, right? Everyone gets a little distracted at home when they're trying to relax after a long day. \n\nAnd with my relationships, like I said before, I really value my friends and family.
